# External Models Registry

**Meta-Universe Specification**

**Document ID:** MU-V2-ECO-008  
**Title:** Registry of External Meta-Models, Standards and Vocabularies  
**Document Class:** Informative  
**Version:** 2.0 (Draft)  
**Status:** Working Draft  
**Normative References:** None  
**Informative References:** [Extension-Model](../02-architecture/Extension-Model.md), [Semantic-Mapping](../03-federation/Semantic-Mapping.md), [Registered-Meta-Models](../06-ecosystem/Registered-Meta-Models.md), [Compatibility-Matrix](../06-ecosystem/Compatibility-Matrix.md)

# 1. Purpose

Meta-Universe does not re-invent the world's existing standards — it **imports and
maps** them ([Extension-Model](../02-architecture/Extension-Model.md) as Semantic
Packages, [Semantic-Mapping](../03-federation/Semantic-Mapping.md) for alignment).
This document is a broad, cross-domain **registry of external meta-models,
standards, ontologies, vocabularies and code lists** that a Meta-Model may import,
extend or map to.

It is a reference catalogue, not a normative list. Inclusion is not endorsement,
and absence is not rejection.

How these standards **connect** — which should be embedded, referenced or mixed in,
and when a concept should be a field rather than a nested model — is governed by
[Meta-Model-Composition](../02-architecture/Meta-Model-Composition.md) and the
curated [Connector-Catalogue](../06-ecosystem/Connector-Catalogue.md).

---

# 2. Field legend

| Field | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| **Group** | Broad domain cluster (see §3). |
| **Name** / **Acronym** | The standard's full and short names. |
| **Specification Source (URL)** | The canonical specification from its maintaining body. |
| **Category** | Ontology · Controlled Vocabulary · Code List · Exchange Format · Schema Language · Modeling Language · Metamodel · Framework · Application Profile · Knowledge Graph · Interface Definition Language · Serialization Format · API Description · Protocol · Messaging Standard · Information Model · Identifier Scheme · Mapping/Lifting · Query Language · Documentation Standard · Metadata Standard · Convention · Terminology · Vocabulary. |
| **Parent Model** | The standard it builds on (`—` if foundational). |
| **Similar Models** | Sibling or alternative standards. |
| **Maintainer** | The standards body / organization. |
| **Format** | The primary serialization or expression form. |
| **Status** | Lifecycle / standing (Active, Recommendation, Draft, Legacy, Withdrawn, …). |
| **Namespace / URI** | Canonical URI prefix or identifier scheme, where one exists. |
| **Year** | First published / latest (where known). |
| **Notes** | One short clause; flags strong **Semantic-Package import candidates** for Meta-Universe. |
| **Compositional Role** | *(CSV only)* The standard's role in composition — R1–R8 — per [Meta-Model-Composition §7](../02-architecture/Meta-Model-Composition.md). See §3a. |
| **Default Link Type** | *(CSV only)* The recommended way to link to it — EMBED · REFERENCE · MIX-IN · COMPOSE · ALIGN · EXTEND · N/A. See §3a. |

---

# 3. Groups and counts

A total of **1180 standards** across 37 domain groups. The catalogue was built in
four passes: three of domain-breadth expansion, then a fourth **source-deepening**
pass that mined the catalogues of the standards bodies already represented here
(W3C, OGC, OASIS, OMG, ISO/IEC, IETF, HL7, GS1, UN/CEFACT, DCMI, SNOMED
International and others) for additional meta-models not yet captured.

| 1 | Semantic Web & Linked Data | 61 |
| 2 | Library, Metadata & Cultural Heritage | 49 |

# 3a. Compositional roles

Every one of the 1180 standards is also classified by its **compositional role** —
how other Meta-Models should connect to it — under the rubric of
[Meta-Model-Composition](../02-architecture/Meta-Model-Composition.md). The role and
its default link type are carried as two extra columns in
[`external-models.csv`](../06-ecosystem/external-models.csv) (they are omitted from
the per-row table below to keep it readable). The curated foundational connectors are
detailed in the [Connector-Catalogue](../06-ecosystem/Connector-Catalogue.md).

| Role | Meaning | Default link | Count |
|------|---------|--------------|------:|
| R1 | Foundational Value-Object | EMBED | 9 |
| R2 | Reference Data / Code List / Classification | REFERENCE | 156 |
| R3 | Identifier Scheme | REFERENCE | 67 |
| R4 | Entity Model | REFERENCE | 140 |
| R5 | Cross-cutting Facet | MIX-IN | 55 |
| R6 | Aggregate / Document / Message | COMPOSE | 263 |
| R7 | Upper Ontology / Foundation / Modeling Substrate | ALIGN | 22 |
| R8 | Infrastructure / Format / Protocol / Tooling | N/A *(or EXTEND for schema languages)* | 468 |
| | **Total** | | **1180** |

By link type: REFERENCE 363 · COMPOSE 263 · N/A 381 · EXTEND 87 · MIX-IN 55 ·
ALIGN 22 · EMBED 9. The classification is informative guidance, produced by a
multi-agent pass and refined by an adversarial review pass that re-examined the 455
most contestable rows and corrected 50 — chiefly demoting domain ontologies,
metamodels and reference models that had been over-assigned to R7, down to R4
(entity models) or R2 (concept schemes). R7 is therefore deliberately small: it is
reserved for genuine upper ontologies (BFO, DOLCE, Common Logic) and the meta-meta
modeling layers (RDF/RDFS/OWL/SKOS-core, MOF/UML/SysML). A standard MAY still be
linked differently in a justified context.

The distribution is itself a finding: genuine **value-objects to embed are rare**
(9) — most reusable structure is concentrated in the curated connectors — while the
bulk of the catalogue is **infrastructure** to express or move data (R8, 468) and
**documents/messages** that compose other models (R6, 263). In other words, most
standards are not things you nest; they are things you *reference*, *compose with*,
or *speak* — which is exactly why a small set of embed/reference connectors does so
much of the integration work.

# 4. Registry

> Standards that legitimately span multiple groups are listed once, under their
> primary group. URLs, versions and status reflect a verified multi-agent sweep
> as of June 2026; verify against the maintaining body before citing. Some
> entries (especially paywalled ISO/IEC, GSMA, NATO and IATA specifications)
> cite the canonical catalogue or landing page rather than the full text.

# 5. Caveats

- Several entries are **provisional or community-track** (e.g. RML, RDF-star, GeoParquet, GeoZarr, OGC API Coverages/Styles, did:web CCG, WebID, DSP, SCITT, MNX, Solid CG).
- Some are **legacy, deprecated or withdrawn** (SWIFT MT, SCORM, FRBR, VPAID, GELLO, CIMI, ISO 21047 ISTC, GS1 EANCOM-era EDIFACT, OStatus, Portable Contacts, UDDI, OpenCyc, SMI, MDDL, RF64).
- Many carry an **ISO/IEC/EN/ITU dual identity** (e.g. BPMN=ISO/IEC 19510, IFC=ISO 16739, KDM=ISO/IEC 19506, AMQP=ISO/IEC 19464, Common Logic=ISO/IEC 24707, BFO=ISO/IEC 21838-2, ODF=ISO/IEC 26300, MQTT=ISO/IEC 20922, OPC UA=IEC 62541, LOIN=ISO 7817-1, DOI=ISO 26324, AIS=ITU-R M.1371).
- The fourth pass deepened **already-listed sources** rather than adding new domains: W3C (R2RML/RML/CSVW/LDP/RIF/VC-DI/RDFC), OGC (GeoPackage/I3S/CityJSON/OGC-API family/SLD/Filter Encoding/Moving Features), OASIS (ODF/DITA/CMIS/ebXML/CIQ/OpenC2/AMQP), OMG (KDM/ASTM/SMM/fUML/KerML/ODM/DOL/LCC/FIGI/SACM), ISO foundational metamodels (Common Logic/PSL/MFI/ISO 15926/PLIB/TBX/LMF/BFO), IETF (SenML/CDDL/JSONPath/Problem Details/RDAP), HL7 (CQL/FSH/FHIRPath/FML/Bulk Data/C-CDA), GS1 (GPC/GenSpecs keys/Web Vocabulary), UN/CEFACT (CCTS/Buy-Ship-Pay RDM/Rec 20/21/24), and ISO code lists/identifiers (639/3166/15924/80000/ISIN/LEI/IBAN/BIC/ISBN/ISSN/ISNI).
- Standards that span domains are listed once under their primary group; "Year" is *first published / latest* where both are known.
- The cleanest **Semantic-Package import candidates** are the RDF/OWL/SKOS-native standards, the OMG OWL reference ontologies (LCC/FIGI/BFO/DTV), the MOF metamodels (KDM/ASTM/SMM/fUML/ODM/KerML), and the foundational logic/process ontologies (Common Logic/PSL/ISO 15926); ~300 rows are flagged as import candidates in their Notes.

# Final Statement

> Meta-Universe is strongest when it stands on what already exists. This registry is the map of that prior art across 37 domains and the standards bodies behind them — the standards a Meta-Model should reach for before inventing anything new.
